5: OUR STORY
6: Changes are afoot in mid-19th century Derbyshire,
7: England. The envy of this part of the county is Alderley
8: Hall, where Margaret, Dowager Countess Fairchild, and
9: her sister-in-law, Ethel, reside with a joint income well in
10: excess of £20,000 pa. Only their loneliness living on such | bsession
11: a sprawling estate dampens the generous hearts of the two
12: widowed and childless ladies. | OThen, to their delight, they welcome new occupants: | Universal
13: dashing nephew Charles and lovely niece Elizabeth, sadly
14: not far removed from tragedy. Their parents, returningfrom holiday in the south of France, perished crossing the | Rules
15: Channel in sudden ill weather. The deaths of Margaret’s
16: brother-in-law and his lady have brought Charles andElizabeth permanently to the Fairchild residence, far from  Compendium
17: their childhood estate in Yorkshire. | A Kayenta Games publicationOf course, the irrepressible Fairchild ladies—now a year | by Dan Hallaganremoved from the terrible accident—know precisely what
18: the best medicine for a sad heart is: romance! Fortunately, | Rulebook Editing | Advisors
19: the surrounding hills are awash in prime matchmaking | Jeff Fraser | David Buckland
20: material. Having endured the hard times of the late 18th | Tim Hallagan | Rulebook Layout
21: century, the respectable families of Derbyshire are intent | Renée Harris | Matt Paquette & Co.
22: on restoring their country estates and reputations, not to | Editorial Review | Appendix Developmentmention finding matches for their marriageable children. | Matt Sumski | Christian LiljebergA sense of optimism is abroad, and the Fairchild ladies | J. Sebastian Pagani
23: have designs! | Artwork | Andrew Riley | Janek Lipiński | Rhiannon Rhodes

228:  | CHAPTER 1. INTEGRATED SETUP
229: 
230:  | This section lists the steps to set up
231:  | the game with or without expansions. | Designer’s Note: | In early 2024, I went to one of my many | Follow the basic Player Setup to set up | Kallaxes and gathered up four boxes for one of my favourite | a standard-length game for 2-4 players
232:  | without any expansions. Follow the | games—the base and three expansions. Friends were an hour
233:  | appropriately labelled extra steps to include | away, and, as the resident rules guru, I needed to brush up. After 10
234:  | expansions and/or common variants. | minutes of flipping through 4 rulebooks, I packed everything up and
235:  | grabbed a different game; I just didn’t have time to go through it all.  TEAMS, CO-OP, AND OTHER
236:  | Later that evening, I realised that I had skipped playing a beloved  VARIANTS
237:  | game because getting it to the table had become unwieldy.
238:  | To set up other official variants, see Chapter 4,
239:  | The Obsession family has 9 rules references scattered over 5 titles! | Optional Rules & Content (pg. 46).
240:  | This compendium is designed to put answers to every rule question | To set up other game modes such as Tableau
241:  | Obsession, Team Obsession or Cooperative | at your fingertips, to rescue Obsession from the very un-Victorian
242:  | Obsession, see Chapter 6, Alternate Game | fate of being…cumbersome.
243:  | Modes (pg. 62).

Except where(C) Characters | Components | otherwise noted, all expansions and optional modules are compatible with each other.(W) Wessex | Feel free to mix and match to create your perfect experience!(B) Promo Tiles Pack

255:  | What is it? An extended game lasts 20 rounds instead of the standard 16.(X) Extended
256:  | What do I need? Just the base game. Compatible with all expansions. | IntegratedIntegrated
257:  | What is it? In solo mode, a single player faces automated opponents of various difficulties.
258: (1P) Solo | What do I need? Just the base game for a standard-length game. An extended game requires | Game | extended solo opponents from Wessex; Upstairs, Downstairs; or Characters.

602:  | CHAPTER 2. PLAYING THE GAME
603:  | 2.1 OVERVIEW
604: 
605:  | In Obsession, each player takes the helm of a 19th-century family striving
606:  | to improve their diminished social standing. Over the course of the game,
607:  | you’ll use your family members and domestic servants to expand your country
608:  | estate, host a variety of influential guests, and strive to secure a match with the
609:  | prestigious Fairchild family. At the end of the game, you score victory points
610:  | (VP) for your progress across a variety of categories. The player who scores the
611:  | most total VP wins the game.
612:  | OUR STORY
613:  | Six of Derbyshire’s respectable
614:  | families—each possessed of eligible | 2.2 SEASONS & ROUNDS | young gentlemen and ladies—find
615:  | themselves embroiled in romance | Game Rounds | and intrigue during a time of | The game is divided into 4 seasons.
616:  | optimism. Though they have | Each season is made up of 3 game rounds,
617:  | long and storied histories in the | followed by a courtship event.
618:  | county, the difficulties of the late | • During each game round, each player takes
619:  | 18th century have left the families | 1 turn, starting with the first player and going
620:  | Asquith, Cavendish, Howard, | clockwise. After the last player’s turn, the
621:  | Ponsonby, Wessex, and York with | round marker advances 1 space.
622:  | neglected country estates and | • | When the marker reaches a courtship event, | tarnished reputations. 1441:  | spaces in and around your country estate. Each player starts with the same 5
1442:  | improvements, and may gain more during the game by buying them from the | CATEGORIES
1443:  | Builders’ Market or the Builders’ Reserve (pg. 40). | Improvement tiles are divided into | Most improvement tiles can be used to host activities during your turn (pg. 17), | 5 categories: Essentials, Service, | while some provide ongoing effects that benefit you throughout the game (pg. 39). | Estate, Prestige, Sporting. 1444:  | For more details on improvement tiles and their effects, | same category typically have similar
1445:  | see Appendix C: Improvement Tiles, pg. 86. | gentry requirements, service needs,
1446:  | and favours. Tile categories are also
1447:  | important during courtship events,
1448:  | when players compare the VP value of
1449:  | IMPROVEMENT TILE ANATOMY | their tiles matching the seasonal theme.

2354:  | CHAPTER 6.
2355:  | ALTERNATE GAME MODES
2356: 
2357:  | These 3 game modes—Tableau, Team, and
2358:  | Cooperative—are not variants, but complete
2359:  | game modes each with a specific emphasis.
2360:  | Tableau Mode—aka Obsession Chess—
2361:  | removes almost all the thematic randomness
2362:  | that represents Victorian fate; Team Mode
2363:  | leverages the thematic fun of partnering with
2364:  | another player; and the Cooperative Mode
2365:  | challenges players to stop the loathsome
2366:  | Sneyd Family from stealing away the lovely
2367:  | Elizabeth.
2368:

2436:  | compete against each other. When scoring objective cards, | • Why use it? Combines elements of cooperative | each team scores their best objectives between their players.

4501: Major Errata
4502: 1.2 Central Play Area Setup, Page 13.
4503: There is a missing (1P) solo play instruction in the (C) SIDEBOARD SETUP section. After the a.
4504: setup step, there should be:
4505: 
4506:  | b. | (1P) Fill Higbee's with 2 SC servants of each type.
4507:  | __________
4508: 
4509: 2.6 Courtship & End of Season, Page 23.
4510: There is a missing (1P) (C) solo play instruction in the ORDER OF PLAY (END OF SEASON) section.
4511: There should be an upkeep step as follows:
4512: 
4513:  | 2.5. | (1P) (C) Add the top 2 cards from the objective deck
4514:  | to Reverend Grantly’s pile, face up.
4515: 
4516: Note: This ensures there are enough objectives to choose from when enjoying the reverend’s
4517: favours.
4518:  | __________
4519: 
4520: 5.3 Solo Estate Challenge Variant, Page 60.
4521: There is an error in the GAME END & SCORING section (bullet points 2 and 3). The correct rules
4522: are:
4523: 
4524:  | • | If you complete your tableau before the target round, you get a bonus. Score 5 VP if you
4525:  | ﬁnish 1 round early. Score 15 VP (5 + 10) if you ﬁnish 2 rounds early. The incremental round
4526:  | bonus increases by 5 VP for every round you ﬁnish early. For example, if you ﬁnish 4 rounds
4527:  | early, your bonus is 50 VP (+5 +10 +15 +20 = 50).
4528:  | • | If you complete your tableau after the target round, you get a penalty. Lose 5 VP if you ﬁnish
4529:  | 1 round late. Lose 15 VP if you ﬁnish 2 rounds late. The incremental round penalty increases
4530:  | by 5 VP for every round you ﬁnish late. For example, if you ﬁnish 4 rounds late, you lose 50
4531:  | VP (–5 –10 –15 –20= –50).
4532:
